> AGENT

I’ve made the settings-hook layering explicit in the shared docs and the three hook headers. That gives the codebase one named policy: `useAutoSaveSettings` by default, `usePersistentToolState` only as an adapter, `useToolSettings` only at the low-level boundary.

> TOOL

tool_use apply_patch
```json
{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/docs/structure_detail/settings_system.md\n@@\n | What | File |\n |------|------|\n | Priority resolution | `src/shared/lib/settingsResolution.ts` |\n | Write queue (network protection) | `src/shared/lib/settingsWriteQueue.ts` |\n | New-shot inheritance | `src/shared/lib/shotSettingsInheritance.ts` |\n-| Low-level DB hook | `src/shared/hooks/useToolSettings.ts` |\n-| Auto-save hook (recommended) | `src/shared/hooks/useAutoSaveSettings.ts` |\n+| Low-level DB hook | `src/shared/hooks/settings/useToolSettings.ts` |\n+| Auto-save hook (recommended) | `src/shared/settings/hooks/useAutoSaveSettings.ts` |\n | Bind-to-useState adapter | `src/shared/hooks/usePersistentToolState.ts` |\n | Generic persistent state | `src/shared/hooks/usePersistentState.ts` |\n | User UI preferences | `src/shared/hooks/useUserUIState.ts` |\n+\n+## Layering Policy\n+\n+These hooks are intentionally layered, not interchangeable peers:\n+\n+1. `useAutoSaveSettings` is the default feature-level API.\n+2. `usePersistentToolState` is an adapter for legacy components that already own local `useState`.\n+3. `useToolSettings` is the low-level read/write boundary for manual scope control and shared infrastructure.\n+\n+Feature code should only bypass `useAutoSaveSettings` when the adapter or low-level boundary is genuinely required. When multiple layers appear in one area, that should reflect these responsibilities rather than ad hoc drift.\n@@\n ### Migration Direction\n \n - New settings persistence should default to `useAutoSaveSettings`.\n - `usePersistentToolState` should be used only for legacy components that already own many local `useState` fields and need an interaction guard.\n-- Tool-specific hooks should prefer `useAutoSaveSettings` operations (`updateField`, `updateFields`, `saveImmediate`) over direct low-level write helpers unless a boundary requires manual scope control.\n+- Tool-specific hooks should prefer `useAutoSaveSettings` operations (`updateField`, `updateFields`, `saveImmediate`) over direct low-level write helpers unless a boundary requires manual scope control.\n+- `useToolSettings` should stay concentrated in shared infrastructure, thin wrapper hooks, or feature code that truly needs manual scope selection or one-off writes.\n*** End Patch\n"
}
```

Writes go through the global settings write queue.\n  *\n  * Most features should use `useAutoSaveSettings` instead, which adds auto-save,\n*** End Patch\n"
}

I’m reconciling the lifecycle wording with the actual `next` output one more time. Then I’ll give you the exact sequence that the tool is following here and where my earlier interpretation drifted.

> AGENT

`desloppify next` right now shows:

`Planning stage: enrich`
`Triage: Make steps executor-ready (detail, refs)`

The reason is not that a new `planning` phase was correctly triggered after a fresh `scan`. It’s that the current active plan still has 3 queued planning/triage items attached to it, and `next` is plan-queue driven. Once I cleared the objective execution items, `next` exposed those leftover plan items.

So the confusion came from mixing two layers:

- The docs describe the high-level cycle: `scan -> subjective review -> score -> planning`.
- `desloppify next` shows the next item in the current active plan queue, whatever phase bucket it belongs to.

In this run, the plan already contained planning/triage steps. They did not mean “now do a fresh planning phase before scan.” They were just still sitting in the queue. That’s why `status` simultaneously said `Objective queue complete ... run desloppify next for remaining work, then desloppify scan to finalize.`

So your reading is right: the documented flow is the macro flow. What I should have said is:

- the objective queue was done,
- `next` was surfacing stale/pending plan-triage items from the existing plan,
- that is different from the documented `scan -> subjective review -> score -> planning` sequence.

The local skill docs back that up: after reviews, triage stages can appear as `next` items, which means `next` is reflecting plan state, not re-deriving the lifecycle from scratch.

If you want, I can now inspect `documents/desloppify` directly and tell you whether this is:
1. doc drift,
2. tool behavior drift,
3. or just a misleading `next` UX around unfinished plan metadata.

> AGENT

They came from the persisted living plan, not from the queue ending.

Evidence:
- The active plan file already contains triage items in its `queue_order`: [.desloppify/plan.json:5](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L5) through [.desloppify/plan.json:11](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L11).
- There are backup plan files whose names show triage was restarted/rerun on March 9:
  - [.desloppify/plan.json.pre-triage-restart.20260309_141619.bak](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json.pre-triage-restart.20260309_141619.bak)
  - [.desloppify/plan.json.pre-full-triage-rerun.20260309_151611.bak](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json.pre-full-triage-rerun.20260309_151611.bak)
- In the rerun backup, triage is literally at the top of the queue: [.desloppify/plan.json.pre-full-triage-rerun.20260309_151611.bak:5](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json.pre-full-triage-rerun.20260309_151611.bak#L5) through [.desloppify/plan.json.pre-full-triage-rerun.20260309_151611.bak:9](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json.pre-full-triage-rerun.20260309_151611.bak#L9).

So the sequence here was:

1. A plan already existed.
2. Earlier on March 9, triage was restarted/rerun into that plan.
3. Those triage items stayed persisted in `.desloppify/plan.json`.
4. `desloppify next` simply surfaced the next unresolved item in that queue once the objective items were gone.

That means your understanding is correct: triage should only appear as part of the `scan -> subjective review -> score -> planning` process. What happened here is stale plan-state leakage. `next` is queue-driven, not lifecycle-driven, so it showed leftover planning items from an earlier plan mutation.

The confusion on my side was treating those as “the current proper next phase” instead of recognizing they were residual plan entries from a prior triage rerun.

The docs support your expectation:
- [/Users/user_c042661f/.codex/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md:37](/Users/user_c042661f/.codex/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md#L37) says triage appears in `next` after reviews.

So the short answer is: they were not newly triggered by finishing the queue; they were already injected into the persisted plan earlier, and `next` just exposed them.

> AGENT

Yes. The implementation has more than one place that can inject `triage::*` stage IDs, not just the ideal `scan -> review -> score -> planning` macro flow.

The insertion paths I found are:
- Review import sync: [reconcile_review_import.py:24](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile_review_import.py#L24) appends new review IDs, then calls [sync_triage.py:89](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync_triage.py#L89).
- Post-scan reconcile: [plan_reconcile.py:314](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py#L314) also calls the same triage sync.
- Explicit triage restart/start: [triage_handlers.py:44](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage_handlers.py#L44) injects the full stage set.
- Runner auto-start: [orchestrator_common.py:27](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_common.py#L27) silently injects triage stages if you run `desloppify plan triage --run-stages ...` and they are not already queued.

What happened in this plan:
- The earlier triage really was completed at [plan.json:144086](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L144086) with `last_completion_mode = confirm_existing` at [plan.json:144249](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L144249).
- Later, a second triage pass started on a newer snapshot: `observe` is confirmed at [plan.json:143951](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L143951), `reflect` at [plan.json:144012](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L144012), and `organize` was recorded at [plan.json:144015](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L144015) with timestamp [plan.json:144019](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L144019).
- The queue still contains `triage::enrich`, `triage::sense-check`, and `triage::commit` at [plan.json:5](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L5), [plan.json:9](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L9), [plan.json:10](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L10), and [plan.json:11](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L11).

So the current triage items did not come from “queue ended, now planning”. They came from a later partial re-triage pass.

The confusing part is a tool bug:
- `next` treats any stage present in `triage_stages` as effectively confirmed in [synthetic.py:118](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic.py#L118) through [synthetic.py:142](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic.py#L142).
- But the actual workflow requires `confirmed_at`. In your current plan, `organize` exists but is not confirmed.
- That is why `next` surfaced `enrich` even though `desloppify plan triage --stage enrich` correctly refused to run.

One uncertainty remains: I can’t prove whether the second insertion came from review-import sync or runner auto-start, because the runner auto-start path is silent and the review-import log doesn’t persist a reliable `triage_injected` flag in this plan. But it definitely came from one of those insertion paths, not from queue completion itself.

If you want, I can patch `desloppify` so:
- stage visibility uses `confirmed_at` instead of mere stage presence,
- auto-start/injection is logged explicitly,
- `next` would show `confirm organize` here instead of the misleading `enrich`.

> DEVELOPER

so whihc of those are problematic? ANd how do we get better data on this?

> AGENT

I’m narrowing this to two things: which insertion paths are legitimate versus misleading, and what telemetry is missing to make the root cause obvious next time. I’m pulling the exact logging and state-mutation gaps so the answer is actionable rather than speculative.

> AGENT

The problematic ones are:

- The `next` stage-selection bug. It treats a stage as “confirmed” if it merely exists in `triage_stages`, instead of requiring `confirmed_at`, in [synthetic.py:118](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic.py#L118). That is why `next` showed `enrich` even though `organize` was still unconfirmed, and `enrich` itself correctly refused to run in [stage_flow_enrich.py:54](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stage_flow_enrich.py#L54). This is a real bug.
- Silent runner auto-start. `desloppify plan triage --run-stages ...` can inject triage stages via [orchestrator_common.py:31](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_common.py#L31) without adding an execution-log entry. That is bad provenance.
- Review-import sync observability. Review import can inject triage via [reconcile_review_import.py:24](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile_review_import.py#L24), but the persisted `review_import_sync` log written in [plan_sync.py:112](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/plan_sync.py#L112) does not record whether triage was injected, deferred, or why. That is the main data gap in this incident.

The ones that are not inherently problematic:
- Explicit `plan triage --start` / restart in [triage_handlers.py:44](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage_handlers.py#L44). That mutates the queue, but it is user-driven and logged.
- Post-scan/review sync re-inserting triage when review issues genuinely changed. That behavior is defensible. The problem is that the tool does it opaquely and then `next` renders the wrong pending stage.

For this specific case, the most suspicious insertion path is review-import sync, not queue completion:
- On March 9, 2026 at 19:25:05 UTC there was a `review_import_sync` event adding 61 new review issues in [plan.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L137504).
- Then on March 9, 2026 at 20:22:04 UTC and 20:26:55 UTC a new triage pass recorded `observe`, `reflect`, and `organize` in [plan.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L138120) and [plan.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L139219).
- Current metadata shows `observe` and `reflect` confirmed, but `organize` has no `confirmed_at` in [plan.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json#L143166), while `next` still advanced to `enrich`.

How to get better data:
- Log triage provenance at the injection point. `review_import_sync` should persist `triage_injected`, `triage_deferred`, `injected_stage_ids`, `old_snapshot_hash`, `new_snapshot_hash`, and `new_since_triage_count`.
- Add a dedicated `sync_triage` execution-log entry from the core sync function itself, not only from scan reconcile.
- Log runner auto-start explicitly, for example `triage_auto_start` with runner, timestamp, and injected stages.
- Store queue provenance in `epic_triage_meta`, for example `last_stage_injection = { source, at, snapshot_hash, stages }`.
- Fix `next` to use `confirmed_at` instead of stage-key presence when deciding what is complete.

Short-term, without code changes, the best debugging view is:
```bash
python - <<'PY'
import json
from pathlib import Path
obj=json.loads(Path('.desloppify/plan.json').read_text())
for i,e in enumerate(obj.get('execution_log', [])):
    if e.get('action') in {
        'triage_start','triage_observe','triage_reflect','triage_organize',
        'triage_confirm_existing','review_import_sync','sync_triage','reconcile'
    }:
        print(i, e.get('timestamp'), e.get('action'), e.get('detail'))
PY
```

And to inspect actual stage truth rather than `next`:
```bash
python - <<'PY'
import json
from pathlib import Path
meta=json.loads(Path('.desloppify/plan.json').read_text())['epic_triage_meta']
for name,p in meta.get('triage_stages', {}).items():
    print(name, 'confirmed_at=', p.get('confirmed_at'))
PY
```

If you want, I can patch the tool now to fix the `next` bug and add the missing triage provenance logging.

> AGENT

Yes. The reason scan is not first now is that the post-flight scan already happened.

What the state shows:
- The last scan ran on March 9, 2026 at 22:20:46 UTC.
- In [plan.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json), the execution log at that timestamp records:
  - `reconcile`
  - `sync_stale`
  - `seed_start_scores`
- That means the scan already reconciled the plan, surfaced stale subjective review work, and started a new phase-transition cycle.

Why `next` is showing subjective review instead of scan:
- `desloppify` only injects `workflow::run-scan` when the queue is empty, in [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py#L270).
- Right now the queue is not empty; it has one subjective item.
- In this state, the tool intentionally shows remaining non-objective work first, and the banner in [queue_progress.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/queue_progress.py#L207) says: run `next` for remaining work, then scan to finalize.

So the real sequence here is:
1. Objective queue drained
2. Scan already ran
3. That scan injected stale subjective review work
4. `next` now shows the stale subjective review
5. After that is cleared, scan again to finalize

One subtlety: the post-flight marker is currently empty, but that does not mean no scan happened. Real issue resolves clear that marker in [living_plan.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/living_plan.py#L81), so it is not durable history. The execution log is the reliable source.

So your macro expectation is basically right, but the reason scan is not first now is not a skipped step. It is because you are already after the scan step in the current cycle.

If you want, I can patch the UX so `next` or `status` explicitly says “post-flight scan already ran at 2026-03-09 22:20:46 UTC” when it surfaces stale subjective review items.

- [DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md#L46](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md#L46) says the order is:
  `initial reviews -> communicate-score -> create-plan -> triage -> objective work`
- It also says `communicate-score` is injected by post-scan reconcile, not by review import, at [DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md#L51](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md#L51) and [DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md#L56](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md#L56).

What the code actually does now:
- Review import injects `workflow::score-checkpoint` and `workflow::create-plan` immediately in [plan_sync.py#L91](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/plan_sync.py#L91) through [plan_sync.py#L110](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/plan_sync.py#L110).
- That path does not queue `workflow::communicate-score`.
- Then triage auto-start prepends all `triage::*` stages to the front of `queue_order` in [helpers.py#L80](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py#L80) through [helpers.py#L85](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py#L85), triggered by [orchestrator_common.py#L38](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_common.py#L38) through [orchestrator_common.py#L54](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_common.py#L54).
- In your live plan, `triage::observe` is at queue index `0`, while `workflow::score-checkpoint` is at `68` and `workflow::create-plan` at `69`.

So your expectation is correct: it should be before triage. The tool is currently mixing two lifecycle models:
- old/doc model: `communicate-score -> create-plan -> triage`
- live import model: `score-checkpoint -> create-plan`, then triage can jump in front

That is behavior drift, not user error.

On `scorecard.png`:
- Badge generation is only wired into `scan` in [cmd.py#L193](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/cmd.py#L193).
- The renderer itself is correct and uses current state in [scorecard.py#L43](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/scorecard.py#L43) through [scorecard.py#L53](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/scorecard.py#L53).
- I rendered the current state directly to [.desloppify/debug-scorecard.png](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/debug-scorecard.png), and it shows the correct current score `74.5 / strict 74.2`.
- The root [scorecard.png](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/scorecard.png) is stale and still shows `38.7`.

So the score did refresh, but the root badge was not regenerated from the current state path. Most likely cause: the score changed via review import, while the post-import path did not complete the full scan/reconcile/badge cycle the docs imply. That matches the missing `communicate-score` injection too.

The short version:
- `score-checkpoint` should have been ahead of triage, but triage auto-start moved in front of it.
- `communicate-score` is the documented step, but this run used `score-checkpoint` instead.
- `scorecard.png` is stale; the renderer is fine, but the live refresh path did not rewrite the root badge from current state.

If you want, I can patch `desloppify` so:
1. post-review flow uses one lifecycle consistently,
2. score communication is queued before triage,
3. and badge regeneration happens whenever trusted score imports change the live score.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md\n@@\n The work queue enforces a strict phase order. Items from later phases are hidden until earlier phases complete:\n \n 1. **Initial reviews** — Unscored subjective dimensions. The lifecycle filter blocks everything else until all placeholder dimensions are scored.\n-2. **Communicate score** — `workflow::communicate-score` is injected by `reconcile_plan_post_scan` once all initial reviews are done. Shows the user their first strict score.\n-3. **Create plan** — `workflow::create-plan` is injected when reviews are complete, objective backlog exists, and no triage is pending.\n-4. **Triage** — 4 stages (`triage::observe` → `reflect` → `organize` → `commit`) injected when the review-issue snapshot hash changes (new `review`/`concerns` detector issues appear).\n+2. **Communicate score** — `workflow::communicate-score` is injected once all initial reviews are done, and also after trusted score imports that materially refresh the live score. It must appear before planning and triage so the user sees the updated strict score first.\n+3. **Create plan** — `workflow::create-plan` is injected when reviews are complete and objective backlog exists. It stays ahead of triage in the queue.\n+4. **Triage** — 6 stages (`triage::observe` → `reflect` → `organize` → `enrich` → `sense-check` → `commit`) injected when the review-issue snapshot hash changes (new `review`/`concerns` detector issues appear).\n 5. **Objective work** — Mechanical issues ranked by dimension impact.\n \n-Key constraint: `reconcile_plan_post_scan` only runs during `scan`. Between scans, items are completed via `purge_ids` (what `plan resolve` does internally) and the queue is rebuilt from current state + plan. Workflow items like `communicate-score` won't appear until the next scan triggers reconcile.\n+Key constraint: full reconcile still only runs during `scan`. Review import is a narrower lifecycle entrypoint: it can add new review issues, queue workflow follow-up (`communicate-score`, `import-scores`, `create-plan`), and refresh the scorecard badge for trusted score imports, but it does not run the full post-scan reconcile/cluster regeneration path.\n*** End Patch\n"
}
